[L6] [BUILD] Created test/harness/docker-compose.yml (docker:28-dind manager/worker1/worker2 on bridge swarmnet, 127.0.0.1:12375-12377, plain TCP no TLS harness-only), test/harness/scripts/harness-up.sh, harness-down.sh, test/harness/README.md, internal/provider/e2e_test.go. gofmt clean; go vet clean for e2e_test.go; bash -n OK on both scripts.
[L6] [TEST] TestE2EConfigRenders PASS (string checks + hclsyntax parse, counts resource blocks). TestAccE2EImageToService SKIPs without TF_ACC, and SKIPs with TF_ACC+DOCKER_HOST but no worker/manager env. Not run against a daemon (none available).
[L6] [BUILD] Ordering deviation from brief: chain is image -> swarm_init -> overlay network (depends_on swarm_init, overlay requires an active swarm) -> 2x swarm_node -> secret -> service (depends_on both nodes). Documented in e2eConfig comment.
[L6] [ASSUMED] docker:28-dind starts with --privileged and DOCKER_TLS_CERTDIR="" and listens on tcp://0.0.0.0:2375; `docker info` healthcheck inside the container goes green once the daemon is up; `docker compose up -d --wait` honours it.
[L6] [ASSUMED] dind nodes have eth0 (swarmnet) plus docker0, so swarm init/join need an explicit advertise_addr; "eth0" is accepted as an interface name by SwarmInit/SwarmJoin. e2e passes advertise_addr=eth0 to docker_swarm_init and both docker_swarm_node resources.
[L6] [ASSUMED] Workers resolve "manager" via the compose network's embedded DNS and reach manager:2377; overlay VXLAN (4789/udp) and gossip (7946) work between privileged dind containers on a bridge network.
[L6] [ASSUMED] The dind daemons have outbound internet: workers pull nginx:1.27-alpine themselves for service tasks (docker_image only pulls it on the manager).
[L6] [ASSUMED] Spread: with 3 replicas, constraint node.role==worker and two workers, swarm's default spread strategy places tasks on both workers (2+1); check waits up to 3m for exactly 3 running tasks on 2 distinct NodeIDs.
[L6] [ASSUMED] docker_network overlay reports scope "swarm"; docker_service state for networks=[network.id] and secrets block round-trips with no drift (PlanOnly step expects empty plan). If Lane 4 normalises network IDs/names or secret file_name differently, this step is the one that will fail.
[L6] [ASSUMED] Destroy order (service -> secret/network/nodes -> swarm_init) leaves manager and both workers in LocalNodeState "inactive"; CheckDestroy asserts that on all three, and checks service/secret NotFound if the manager swarm is still active.

[L3] [ASSUMED] Not run against a daemon: SwarmInit/SwarmJoin/SwarmLeave/NodeUpdate/NodeRemove/SwarmUpdate behaviour, response shapes and error texts are taken from docker v28.5.2 client source and Engine API docs, never observed live.
[L3] [ASSUMED] Version conflicts on NodeUpdate are detected by the error text "update out of sequence" (swarmkit) or "version mismatch"; retried up to 5 times with a fresh NodeInspect.
[L3] [ASSUMED] SwarmLeave on a non-member returns an error containing "not part of a swarm" (treated as success); a joining node daemon that is unreachable at destroy (client.IsErrConnectionFailed) is skipped and the node is still force-removed from the manager once it reports down.
[L3] [ASSUMED] The manager reports a force-left node as Status.State=down within 2m (swarmLeaveTimeout); join waits up to 2m for Info().Swarm.LocalNodeState=active and for the manager to see the node.
[L3] [ASSUMED] docker_swarm_init: the daemon does not report advertise_addr/listen_addr back, so they are kept from config/state; import sets listen_addr to the default 0.0.0.0:2377 and leaves advertise_addr null (ImportStateVerifyIgnore). default_addr_pool/subnet_size/data_path_port are Optional+Computed and read from SwarmInspect (daemon defaults 10.0.0.0/8, 24, 4789).
[L3] [ASSUMED] docker_swarm_init: a locked manager (autolock after restart) keeps state with a warning instead of being removed. autolock is the only in-place update (SwarmUpdate); added computed sensitive `unlock_key` (SwarmGetUnlockKey) beyond the contract.
[L3] [ASSUMED] docker_swarm_node import: ImportState sets a private-state flag so the first apply that fills in host/join_token/manager_address/advertise_addr/listen_addr updates state in place instead of replacing; the flag is cleared on Update. Plan-time behaviour of this RequiresReplaceIf+private-state path was not exercised by a test.
[L3] [ASSUMED] docker_swarm_node Create adopts a daemon that is already an active member the manager knows (idempotent retry); a member of another swarm is an error. The joining node's client uses API version negotiation and the provider's user agent; no api_version attribute on the resource.
[L3] [ASSUMED] Node labels are written as given (no managed-by label added, since the node itself is not created by the provider); labels set outside Terraform are removed when `labels` is managed. `labels = {}` is preserved as an empty map.

[L1] [ASSUMED] docker_image build uses the classic builder (BuilderV1) via ImageBuild; BuildKit-only Dockerfile features (RUN --mount, heredocs needing BuildKit) are not supported. Built image ID taken from the build aux {"ID"} message, falling back to inspecting the tag. Not run against a live daemon.
[L1] [ASSUMED] build.context / dockerfile are resolved relative to the provider process cwd (Terraform's working directory); docs say to use path.module. build.dockerfile is relative to the context (or absolute); a Dockerfile outside the context is injected as .dockerfile.<hash> like the CLI.
[L1] [ASSUMED] context_digest is computed in ModifyPlan; if the context cannot be read at plan time (e.g. generated during apply) it plans unknown, and for an existing resource that forces replacement (conservative).
[L1] [ASSUMED] docker_image destroy untags `name` when it still points at image_id (image deleted once untagged), else removes image_id; PruneChildren=true; not-found is ignored.
[L1] [ASSUMED] docker_registry_image: id = name; digest from push aux {"Digest"}, else DistributionInspect. Read refreshes sha256_digest via DistributionInspect and drops state on not-found/"manifest unknown". Push retries skip errors that look like auth/denied/no-such-image.
[L1] [ASSUMED] keep_remotely=false deletes via registry HTTP API v2 (DELETE /v2/<repo>/manifests/<digest>) from the provider using registry_auth creds (Basic or Bearer token, scope repository:<repo>:delete,pull); https first, http fallback only for localhost/loopback; Docker Hub maps to registry-1.docker.io (Hub usually refuses manifest deletes -> warning). Failure = warning + removed from state.

[L2] [ASSUMED] docker_container Read subtracts image defaults: env/labels/cmd/entrypoint/user/working_dir/healthcheck are reported only when they differ from ImageInspect(container.Image).Config or the key/value is already in prior state/plan; if the image cannot be inspected only keys already in state are reported (import still reports everything).
[L2] [ASSUMED] Daemon stores HostConfig.PortBindings, Mounts, Ulimits, CapAdd/CapDrop and EndpointSettings.IPAMConfig exactly as requested; Read uses those (not NetworkSettings.Ports) so ephemeral host ports are not stored in state. Not verified against a live daemon.
[L2] [ASSUMED] Daemon may merge default-ulimits into HostConfig.Ulimits, so Read reports only ulimit names already in state (all on import). Capability names are compared case-insensitively without the CAP_ prefix.
[L2] [ASSUMED] Endpoint Aliases may include container name/ID/short ID (older API); those are filtered. The default bridge/host/none network implied by NetworkMode is not reported in networks_advanced.
[L2] [ASSUMED] memory_swap is Computed (daemon sets 2x memory when unset); on a memory change with memory_swap unset, Update sends MemorySwap = 2*memory. Removing memory/cpu_shares/nano_cpus forces replacement because ContainerUpdate treats 0 as "unchanged".
[L2] [ASSUMED] docker_network Read reports gateway/ip_range only if set in config, IPAM pools only for subnets in config, and driver options only for keys in config (daemon-assigned values ignored); import reports everything.
[L2] [ASSUMED] docker_volume Create refuses a name that already exists (VolumeCreate would silently adopt it) and tells the user to import instead.
[L2] [ASSUMED] docker_container pulls the image (with registry_auth) when ImageInspect says not found; `running` is planned true when must_run, so a stopped container is started by the next apply.
[L2] [TODO] Acceptance tests (TestAccContainer_basic, TestAccNetwork_basic, TestAccVolume_basic, data sources) never run here (no daemon); run with TF_ACC=1 DOCKER_HOST=... to verify inspect round trips, especially healthcheck/log_opts/memory_swap and ImportStateVerify.

TestAcc{Secret,DockerConfig,Service,Stack}Basic SKIP without TF_ACC/DOCKER_HOST. Full package go test also ok.
[L4] [ASSUMED] Daemon round-trip normalisation taken from daemon/cluster/convert in docker v28.5.2 source: port protocol ""->tcp, publish_mode ""->ingress, endpoint mode ""->vip, restart MaxAttempts always returned, update failure_action/order zero values read back as pause/stop-first. Flatten is prior-aware to absorb these, plus the client's @sha256 digest pin and Placement.Platforms. Verified only against the fake daemon, not a live Swarm.
[L4] [ASSUMED] Overlay networks report option com.docker.network.driver.overlay.vxlanid_list; docker_stack drops it on read.
[L4] [ASSUMED] TaskList with filter service=<id> errors once the service is gone (daemon/cluster/tasks.go), so removal waits list all tasks and filter by ServiceID client-side.
[L4] [ASSUMED] converge_config absent = no wait (matches kreuzwerker). Global mode converges when every task with DesiredState=running is running. An UpdateStatus whose StartedAt predates the service's UpdatedAt is treated as stale (so an old rollback does not fail a label-only update).
[L4] [ASSUMED] docker_config import fills data from the daemon (configs are readable); docker_secret import leaves data null, so the next plan replaces the secret unless config data matches nothing -> documented in import.sh.
[L4] [ASSUMED] Secret/config refs: file_name defaults to the reference as written when it is a name (stack short name -> /run/secrets/<short>, like docker stack), otherwise the object's name; uid/gid default "0", mode default 292 (0444). max_failure_ratio is float64 in HCL, float32 on the wire; flatten keeps the configured value when float32-equal.
[L4] [ASSUMED] docker_stack: services attach only to the networks listed (no implicit <stack>_default network); every stack service gets a network alias of its short name. Stack volumes are never created/removed by the provider (tasks create them per node through mount VolumeOptions); on read, mounts pointing at <stack>_<vol> are mapped back to the short name and their VolumeOptions dropped, so volume_* attributes on such mounts are ignored.
[L4] [ASSUMED] docker_stack update: when any stack network/secret/config is replaced, every service is re-submitted via ServiceUpdate (only its users are removed+recreated); unchanged services are otherwise skipped by comparing plan vs prior state. Imported stacks list services/networks/secrets/configs sorted by name and secret data null (first apply after import replaces stack secrets).
[L4] [TODO] Acceptance tests (TestAcc*) not run: no live Swarm on this machine. Run with TF_ACC=1 DOCKER_HOST=<swarm manager>.
[L4] [TODO] docker_service does not expose hostname, user, dir, hosts, dns, cap_add/drop, sysctls, ulimits, stop_grace_period, force_update, job modes (replicated-job/global-job) or per-network aliases; add if needed.
[L4] [ASSUMED] (correction to the docker_config/docker_secret import line above) After importing docker_secret, state has no data, so the next plan replaces the secret; examples/resources/docker_secret/import.sh says so.

## Final summary

### Per-lane status

| Lane | Scope | Status |
|---|---|---|
| L0 scaffold | go.mod, main.go, provider.go (host/api_version/tls/registry_auth), client.go, convert.go, GNUmakefile, .goreleaser.yml, manifest, tools.go, README | Done. Builds, 5 unit tests. |
| L1 image | docker_image (build/pull, .dockerignore tar, context_digest), docker_registry_image (push w/ retries), data docker_image, data docker_registry_image | Done. 23 unit tests; acc tests unrun. |
| L2 primitives | docker_network, docker_volume, docker_container; data docker_network, docker_volume | Done. 21 unit tests incl. plan-only terraform run and fake daemon; acc tests unrun. |
| L3 swarm topology | docker_swarm_init, docker_swarm_node; data docker_swarm_tokens, docker_node, docker_nodes | Done. 23 unit tests vs fake Engine API; acc tests unrun. |
| L4 swarm workload | docker_secret, docker_config, docker_service (converge), docker_stack (composite, no compose/CLI) | Done. 26 tests incl. full terraform CRUD/import vs fake Engine API; acc tests unrun. |
| L5 docs + skill | templates/, tools/genskill, skills/terraform-provider-docker (SKILL.md + 19 reference files), docs/ | Done. Generated from live schema; validate passes. |
| L6 harness | test/harness (dind manager + 2 workers, up/down scripts), internal/provider/e2e_test.go | Done. Config-render unit test passes; e2e unrun. |

Every resource has full CRUD + ImportState, RequiresReplace where Docker cannot mutate, an acceptance test file, and examples/resources/<name>/{resource.tf,import.sh}. No os/exec in provider or tools; the only compose file is test/harness/docker-compose.yml.

- [L1] [ASSUMED] build.context / dockerfile are resolved relative to the provider process cwd (Terraform's working directory); docs say to use path.module. build.dockerfile is relative to the context (or absolute); a Dockerfile outside the context is injected as .dockerfile.<hash> like the CLI.
- [L1] [ASSUMED] context_digest is computed in ModifyPlan; if the context cannot be read at plan time (e.g. generated during apply) it plans unknown, and for an existing resource that forces replacement (conservative).
- [L1] [ASSUMED] docker_image destroy untags `name` when it still points at image_id (image deleted once untagged), else removes image_id; PruneChildren=true; not-found is ignored.
- [L1] [ASSUMED] docker_registry_image: id = name; digest from push aux {"Digest"}, else DistributionInspect. Read refreshes sha256_digest via DistributionInspect and drops state on not-found/"manifest unknown". Push retries skip errors that look like auth/denied/no-such-image.
- [L1] [ASSUMED] keep_remotely=false deletes via registry HTTP API v2 (DELETE /v2/<repo>/manifests/<digest>) from the provider using registry_auth creds (Basic or Bearer token, scope repository:<repo>:delete,pull); https first, http fallback only for localhost/loopback; Docker Hub maps to registry-1.docker.io (Hub usually refuses manifest deletes -> warning). Failure = warning + removed from state.
- [L5] [ASSUMED] Forces-replacement is detected from description text ("forces a new resource" / "forces replacement" / "forces a replacement" / "force a new resource", case-insensitive) since the protocol schema carries no plan modifiers; an attribute with RequiresReplace but no such sentence is missed.
- [L5] [ASSUMED] Reference file names: reference/<type>.md for resources, reference/data.<type>.md for data sources (resource and data source share names, e.g. docker_image), reference/provider.md for the provider block.
- [L5] [ASSUMED] Worked example sets docker_service.image = docker_image.web.repo_digest, assuming repo_digest is a pullable `name@sha256:...` RepoDigests entry; and passes tls via file() (PEM content) rather than relying on `~` expansion.
- [L5] [ASSUMED] Stale-file removal only deletes reference/*.md files carrying the generator marker comment; hand-written files there survive.
- [L2] [ASSUMED] docker_container Read subtracts image defaults: env/labels/cmd/entrypoint/user/working_dir/healthcheck are reported only when they differ from ImageInspect(container.Image).Config or the key/value is already in prior state/plan; if the image cannot be inspected only keys already in state are reported (import still reports everything).
- [L2] [ASSUMED] Daemon stores HostConfig.PortBindings, Mounts, Ulimits, CapAdd/CapDrop and EndpointSettings.IPAMConfig exactly as requested; Read uses those (not NetworkSettings.Ports) so ephemeral host ports are not stored in state. Not verified against a live daemon.
- [L2] [ASSUMED] Daemon may merge default-ulimits into HostConfig.Ulimits, so Read reports only ulimit names already in state (all on import). Capability names are compared case-insensitively without the CAP_ prefix.
- [L2] [ASSUMED] Endpoint Aliases may include container name/ID/short ID (older API); those are filtered. The default bridge/host/none network implied by NetworkMode is not reported in networks_advanced.
- [L2] [ASSUMED] memory_swap is Computed (daemon sets 2x memory when unset); on a memory change with memory_swap unset, Update sends MemorySwap = 2*memory. Removing memory/cpu_shares/nano_cpus forces replacement because ContainerUpdate treats 0 as "unchanged".
- [L2] [ASSUMED] docker_network Read reports gateway/ip_range only if set in config, IPAM pools only for subnets in config, and driver options only for keys in config (daemon-assigned values ignored); import reports everything.
- [L2] [ASSUMED] docker_volume Create refuses a name that already exists (VolumeCreate would silently adopt it) and tells the user to import instead.
- [L2] [ASSUMED] docker_container pulls the image (with registry_auth) when ImageInspect says not found; `running` is planned true when must_run, so a stopped container is started by the next apply.
- [L4] [ASSUMED] Daemon round-trip normalisation taken from daemon/cluster/convert in docker v28.5.2 source: port protocol ""->tcp, publish_mode ""->ingress, endpoint mode ""->vip, restart MaxAttempts always returned, update failure_action/order zero values read back as pause/stop-first. Flatten is prior-aware to absorb these, plus the client's @sha256 digest pin and Placement.Platforms. Verified only against the fake daemon, not a live Swarm.
- [L4] [ASSUMED] Overlay networks report option com.docker.network.driver.overlay.vxlanid_list; docker_stack drops it on read.
- [L4] [ASSUMED] TaskList with filter service=<id> errors once the service is gone (daemon/cluster/tasks.go), so removal waits list all tasks and filter by ServiceID client-side.
- [L4] [ASSUMED] converge_config absent = no wait (matches kreuzwerker). Global mode converges when every task with DesiredState=running is running. An UpdateStatus whose StartedAt predates the service's UpdatedAt is treated as stale (so an old rollback does not fail a label-only update).
- [L4] [ASSUMED] docker_config import fills data from the daemon (configs are readable); docker_secret import leaves data null, so the next plan replaces the secret unless config data matches nothing -> documented in import.sh.
- [L4] [ASSUMED] Secret/config refs: file_name defaults to the reference as written when it is a name (stack short name -> /run/secrets/<short>, like docker stack), otherwise the object's name; uid/gid default "0", mode default 292 (0444). max_failure_ratio is float64 in HCL, float32 on the wire; flatten keeps the configured value when float32-equal.
- [L4] [ASSUMED] docker_stack: services attach only to the networks listed (no implicit <stack>_default network); every stack service gets a network alias of its short name. Stack volumes are never created/removed by the provider (tasks create them per node through mount VolumeOptions); on read, mounts pointing at <stack>_<vol> are mapped back to the short name and their VolumeOptions dropped, so volume_* attributes on such mounts are ignored.
- [L4] [ASSUMED] docker_stack update: when any stack network/secret/config is replaced, every service is re-submitted via ServiceUpdate (only its users are removed+recreated); unchanged services are otherwise skipped by comparing plan vs prior state. Imported stacks list services/networks/secrets/configs sorted by name and secret data null (first apply after import replaces stack secrets).
- [L4] [ASSUMED] (correction to the docker_config/docker_secret import line above) After importing docker_secret, state has no data, so the next plan replaces the secret; examples/resources/docker_secret/import.sh says so.

### Full e2e once a Docker host is available

On a Linux/macOS host (or WSL) with Docker that allows privileged containers, from the repo root:

```
make e2e
```

Without make:

```
bash test/harness/scripts/harness-up.sh && source test/harness/.env && TF_ACC=1 go test ./internal/provider/ -run TestAccE2E -count=1 -v -timeout 30m; bash test/harness/scripts/harness-down.sh
```

All per-resource acceptance tests against the same harness (manager + worker env from .env):

```
bash test/harness/scripts/harness-up.sh && source test/harness/.env && TF_ACC=1 go test ./internal/provider/ -run TestAcc -count=1 -v -timeout 60m; bash test/harness/scripts/harness-down.sh
```

Session = session.NewSession(sharedKey=sha256(abs context)) + secretsprovider.NewStore + an auth.AuthServer (Credentials only, from registry_auth; token-authority RPCs Unimplemented so BuildKit fetches tokens itself), run via client.DialHijack(ctx, "/session", "h2c", meta). One element maps to ImageBuildOptions.Platform; build.platform + build.platforms together is an error.

All new container attributes RequiresReplace ("Forces replacement.").
[P2-C] [ASSUMED] docker_service: ContainerSpec Hostname/User/Dir/Hosts/DNSConfig/CapabilityAdd/Drop/Sysctls/Ulimits/StopGracePeriod round-trip verbatim (daemon/cluster/convert/container.go). hosts takes "host:ip" and is sent in swarmkit "ip host" form (entries containing a space pass through); read converts back, one "host:ip" per hostname of a multi-alias entry. Capability names compared ignoring case/CAP_ prefix. All update in place via ServiceUpdate.
[P2-C] [ASSUMED] docker_service job modes: daemon fills ReplicatedJob MaxConcurrent=1 and TotalCompletions=MaxConcurrent when unset (convert/service.go), so those read back null when unset and equal to the defaults. update_config/rollback_config with a job mode is an error (docker CLI rule "not supported for jobs"). The Engine rejects any mode-type change, so docker_service keeps RequiresReplace on every mode change. Converge: replicated_job done when Complete tasks of the current JobStatus.JobIteration >= total_completions; global_job when all current-iteration tasks are terminal and >=1 completed; failed tasks are not fatal (restart policy) but are listed on timeout. Verified only against the fake daemon.
[P2-C] [ASSUMED] force_update: set -> sent as TaskTemplate.ForceUpdate; unset -> the daemon's counter is preserved on ServiceUpdate and ignored on refresh (no drift from `docker service update --force`). networks_advanced and networks are mutually exclusive (plan-time validator on the block, works inside a stack service block); on import, attachments land in networks_advanced only if one has aliases. In a stack the short service name is always added as an alias and stripped on read unless the user listed it.

The BuildKit dockerfile frontend got the raw PAX-format context tar as the Dockerfile: with Version=BuilderBuildKit the tar request body is not unpacked as the context, so [P2-B] ASSUMED "BuildKit path keeps sending the context as the tar request body (no filesync)" is broken against this daemon (or the PAX tar header format is the trigger). Seen once while Lane B was still editing; Lane B to re-check with: pwsh -NoProfile -File test/harness/Invoke-E2E.ps1 -Run TestAccImageBuildKit

[P2-ORCH] [FIXED] [P2-B] BuildKit context tar: TestAccImageBuildKit failed live with "dockerfile parse error on line 1: unknown instruction: PaxHeaders.0/Dockerfile...". Root cause: imgTarHeader (image_build.go) forced tar.FormatPAX but kept sub-second ModTime, so Go wrote a PAX mtime record ('x' block) before every entry; BuildKit sniffs the first block of the uploaded body to tell a context archive from a bare Dockerfile, failed on the PAX record and parsed the whole tar as the Dockerfile (classic builds do not sniff, so they passed). Fix (3 lines, mirrors moby archive.FileInfoHeader): ModTime truncated to the second, AccessTime/ChangeTime zeroed. Regression test TestImageTarFirstBlockIsPlainHeader (sub-second mtime fixture) fails without the fix ("first tar block is a PAX header (typeflag 'x')") and passes with it. The [P2-B] assumption that the request body is used as the BuildKit context was right; the tar format was the trigger.

## Pass 3

Goal: parity with kreuzwerker/docker v4.6.0, the Terraform 1.14 surface (actions, ephemeral resources, provider functions, list resources), the Pass 2 verification gaps, and the skill kept in sync. Nothing is committed.

### Log

[P3-0] [BUILD] terraform-plugin-framework v1.19.0 (already pinned; the newest release, and it already ships the action, ephemeral, function and list packages), terraform-plugin-go v0.31.0 (newest), terraform-plugin-mux v0.23.1 (added, newest), golang.org/x/crypto v0.58.0 (was indirect v0.57.0), github.com/docker/cli v29.9.3+incompatible (was indirect v29.8.2), github.com/compose-spec/compose-go/v2 v2.16.2 (added). All pinned in tools/tools.go.
[P3-0] [FIXED] Pinning docker/cli v28.5.2 to match docker/docker v28.5.2 made go downgrade moby/buildkit v0.34.2 => v0.26.3 (buildkit 0.34 needs docker/cli >= v29.8.2). Switched to docker/cli v29.9.3 and put buildkit back at v0.34.2. docker/cli v29 brings in github.com/moby/moby/client v0.6.2 + api v1.56.1 next to github.com/docker/docker v28.5.2. Both are fine in one build.
[P3-0] [BUILD] provider.go now implements ProviderWithActions, ProviderWithEphemeralResources, ProviderWithFunctions and ProviderWithListResources. Configure hands the same *providerData to EphemeralResourceData, ActionData and ListResourceData. Each lane gets its own register file (register_composefile.go B, register_p3_container.go C, register_actions.go D, register_ephemeral.go / register_functions.go / register_lists.go / register_p3_datasources.go E), so no lane needs to edit provider.go to register anything.
[P3-0] [BUILD] examples/provider/provider.tf has required_version = ">= 1.14.0".
[P3-0] [TEST] go mod tidy, go build ./..., go vet ./... clean; go test ./... -count=1 PASS (internal/provider 30.3s, tools/genskill 0.7s).
[P3-0] [ASSUMED] The local terraform CLI is v1.9.8. Acceptance tests for actions, list resources and write-only arguments need >= 1.14 (ephemeral needs >= 1.10). Lane F has the harness export TF_ACC_TERRAFORM_VERSION so terraform-plugin-testing installs a 1.14 binary, and each such test also calls tfversion.SkipBelow.
[P3-0] [BUILD] Each lane runs in its own git worktree (C:\__Code\tpd-wt\lane<X>, detached at a03f6f4 plus the Lane 0 changes). Every lane edits only `internal/provider`, so a broken half-written file in one lane would otherwise break `go test` for all the others. Lanes own disjoint files, so the orchestrator merges by copying them back.
[P3-A] [BUILD] context attribute: resolved with docker/cli cli/context/store (+ cli/context TLS loader) from <DOCKER_CONFIG or ~/.docker>/contexts; supplies host, ca/cert/key and SkipTLSVerify (new endpointConfig.SkipTLSVerify); an ssh:// context host maps onto the pure-Go ssh transport. Precedence: host > ssh.host > context attr > DOCKER_HOST > DOCKER_CONTEXT > config.json currentContext > unix:///var/run/docker.sock; "default" = no-op (falls back to DOCKER_HOST/default socket). cli/context/docker is NOT imported (it links docker/cli connhelper, which shells out to ssh); the store is typed with context.EndpointMetaBase directly. (internal/provider/provider_context.go, provider.go resolveEndpoint)
[P3-A] [BUILD] disable_docker_daemon_check (bool, default false): when false Configure pings GET /_ping once (5s timeout + ssh.connect_timeout per hop for SSH hosts) and turns a failure into a WARNING diagnostic, never an error; validate does not call Configure, plan never fails on it. The client stays lazy either way.
[P3-A] [BUILD] cert_path (dir with ca.pem/cert.pem/key.pem) beside the tls block; mutual exclusion enforced at plan time by dockerProvider.ValidateConfig (ProviderWithValidateConfig) via validateProviderModel, which also checks ssh.connect_timeout and ssh.proxy_jump syntax. TLS precedence: tls block > cert_path > context TLS > DOCKER_TLS_VERIFY+DOCKER_CERT_PATH.
[P3-A] [BUILD] registry_auth: auth_disabled, config_file_content, password_wo (conflicts with password via stringvalidator.ConflictsWith), config_file conflicts with config_file_content; DOCKER_REGISTRY_USER/DOCKER_REGISTRY_PASS defaults (only when the entry names no config file); default config = DOCKER_CONFIG (dir -> dir/config.json, or a file) else ~/.docker/config.json; config files parsed by docker/cli cli/config.LoadFromReader; lookup order credHelpers[registry] > credsStore > auths, like the docker CLI; identity tokens carried in new registryCredential.IdentityToken and sent by registryAuthConfigFor. (internal/provider/provider_auth.go; old resolveRegistryAuth/credentialFromConfigFile removed from client.go, credentialFromConfigFile kept as a wrapper)
[P3-A] [ASSUMED] Credential helpers (docker-credential-<name>) are run by docker/cli's credentials.NewNativeStore: the one documented exception to "nothing new shells out". Gated behind provider attribute allow_credential_helpers (default false); when false no helper is ever started, an auths entry is still used, and a helper-only registry errors naming allow_credential_helpers. Configure logs a tflog.Info line when the gate is on.
[P3-A] [ASSUMED] Provider-level password_wo (registry_auth.password_wo, ssh.password_wo) cannot carry WriteOnly: true: terraform-plugin-framework v1.19.0 provider/schema has no WriteOnly field (IsWriteOnly always false; provider config is never persisted in state). They are Sensitive, mutually exclusive with password, and documented as such. docker_swarm_node's ssh.password_wo IS a real WriteOnly attribute (resource schema).
[P3-A] [BUILD] ssh block (provider + docker_swarm_node), pure Go golang.org/x/crypto/ssh: user, host, port (22), identity_file (PEM or path; default ~/.ssh/id_ed25519|id_ecdsa|id_rsa if present+unencrypted), identity_agent (SSH_AUTH_SOCK on unix; \\.\pipe\openssh-ssh-agent via go-winio on Windows, or SSH_AUTH_SOCK when it names a pipe), password, password_wo, known_hosts_file (default ~/.ssh/known_hosts), strict_host_key_checking (default true; false = InsecureIgnoreHostKey), proxy_jump (user@host:port, comma-separated chain, each hop via direct-tcpip and host-key checked), connect_timeout (10s, bounds TCP+handshake per hop), remote_socket (/var/run/docker.sock). Docker client: WithHTTPClient + WithHost("unix://"+remote_socket) (dummy api.moby.localhost host, no proxy) + WithDialContext(ssh dialer); the dialer reuses one pooled connection chain per distinct sshConfig and opens a direct-streamlocal@openssh.com channel per HTTP connection, reconnecting once if the pooled connection died. (internal/provider/provider_ssh.go, provider_ssh_agent_unix.go, provider_ssh_agent_windows.go, client.go newDockerClient)

Attributes: content | files (exactly one, resourcevalidator.ExactlyOneOf), project_name (Optional+Computed, RequiresReplaceIfConfigured + ModifyPlan RequiresReplace when the parsed name changes), profiles, env (sensitive), env_file, working_dir, mode (local|swarm, default local, RequiresReplace), wait (default true), wait_timeout (default 2m), keep_volumes (default false); computed id, spec_hash, services (map(list(string))), networks, volumes. Import ID "<project>" or "<project>:swarm".
[P3-B] [BUILD] Parsing: compose-go v2.16.2 loader.LoadWithContext with a ConfigDetails built only from env_file (dotenv.ReadFile, later wins) + env (wins); no OS environment, no .env auto-load, no remote loaders. Relative paths resolve against working_dir, else the first file's dir, else os.Getwd() (Terraform's working dir). Project name: project_name, else YAML name:, else NormalizeProjectName(base dir). project.WithoutUnnecessaryResources() is applied so networks/volumes/secrets/configs no enabled service uses are not created (matches `docker compose up`).
[P3-B] [BUILD] Local mode: containers <project>-<service>-<n> (container_name honoured, error with replicas>1), networks/volumes named by compose-go (<project>_<key>), labels com.docker.compose.project/service/container-number/oneoff=False/project.working_dir/network/volume + io.terraform.provider + com.docker.compose.config-hash (sha256 of the expanded create request). Services created in depends_on order; service_healthy / service_completed_successfully waited (wait_timeout); network_mode/ipc/pid service:x -> container:<project>-x-1; volumes_from mapped; first network at create, others via NetworkConnect; aliases = service name + configured aliases. Secrets/configs: file source -> read-only bind mount; content/environment source -> CopyToContainer tar before start (uid/gid/mode honoured); external -> plan-time error.
[P3-B] [BUILD] Swarm mode: compose -> swarm.ServiceSpec with name references (Target/SecretName/ConfigName) resolved to IDs at apply via svcLoadLookup; reuses stackListExisting, stackPlanObjects (prior fingerprints = live config-hash labels), stackPlanServices (mode-type change / replaced object -> recreate), stackRemoveServices, stackRemoveRetry, stackDestroy, svcUpdate, svcWaitConverged, svcImageMatches, svcModeOf. Objects labelled com.docker.stack.namespace=<project>; services named <project>_<service>; network driver default overlay, scope swarm.
[P3-B] [BUILD] Drift: Read re-parses the stored inputs and compares with the daemon: missing objects, config-hash label mismatch, orphans with the project label, containers exited non-zero (or stopped while restart always/unless-stopped; restart "no" + exit 0 is not drift), swarm service image (digest-pinned compare) / mode / replicas. Drift sets spec_hash = "drifted: <up to 5 reasons>" so the plan shows an in-place update; Update recreates changed containers (and restart:true dependents, and containers on a replaced network), starts stopped ones, removes orphans. Volumes whose definition changed are kept with a warning (never dropped). Delete removes everything labelled with the project (volumes unless keep_volumes).

Environment-sourced secrets become sensitive `variable` blocks + base64encode(var.X); file sources filebase64("${path.module}/..."); bind sources abspath("${path.module}/..."); local replicas -> count with name "<p>-<s>-${count.index + 1}"; depends_on -> meta-argument.

[P3-C] [BUILD] docker_container upload blocks (new internal/provider/container_upload.go): file, content | content_base64 | source (+ source_hash), executable, permissions (octal string), owner, group. Each upload is a one-file in-memory archive/tar (PAX, Uid/Gid from owner/group, mode 0644 / 0744 executable / permissions) sent with CopyToContainer to dir(file) after ContainerCreate + NetworkConnect and before ContainerStart. All tars are built before ContainerCreate so a missing source file fails early. Read keeps the prior upload list (not readable from the daemon).
[P3-C] [BUILD] upload plan modifier containerUploadReplaceIf (listplanmodifier.RequiresReplaceIf): a changed upload forces replacement unless plan must_run is false and state running is false; then Update re-copies every upload into the stopped container in place (no start). Validators: content has stringvalidator.ExactlyOneOf(content_base64, source); executable has boolvalidator.ConflictsWith(permissions) and permissions has stringvalidator.ConflictsWith(executable); file must be absolute; permissions must match ^0?[0-7]{3,4}$.
[P3-C] [BUILD] docker_container mounts.selinux_relabel ("z" or "Z", OneOf validator). mount.Mount has no relabel field, so such an entry is sent as a HostConfig.Binds string "<src>:<dst>:<rw|ro>,<z|Z>" (bind needs an absolute source, volume a named source, tmpfs rejected). Read merges HostConfig.Binds back into mounts (ro -> read_only, z/Z -> selinux_relabel, a source without leading / is a volume). containerFlattenMounts gained a binds parameter; containerExpandMountsBinds is the new expander (containerExpandMounts kept as a wrapper).
[P3-C] [ASSUMED] The daemon stores HostConfig.Binds verbatim (no normalisation of "rw,z") so selinux_relabel round-trips without drift, and CopyToContainer keeps tar header Uid/Gid when copyUIDGID is not set (daemon extracts with Lchown from the headers). Not verified live.
[P3-C] [ASSUMED] Side effect: imported containers created with `-v` binds now report them as mounts entries (HostConfig.Binds was ignored before). Containers using only Mounts are unaffected (Binds is nil).
[P3-C] [BUILD] docker_tag (new tag_resource.go): source_image, target_image (both RequiresReplace), source_image_id and id ("<source_image_id>.<target_image>") computed. Create: ImageInspect(source) then ImageTag(sourceID, target). Read: inspects source (falls back to the recorded ID if the source is gone) and target; target gone or pointing at a different ID -> RemoveResource so the next apply re-tags. Delete: ImageRemove(target tag, Force=false, PruneChildren=false) only while the target still points at our ID; never removes by ID and never touches the source. When the target is the image's only tag (source given as an ID), ImageRemove would delete the image, so the tag is kept and a warning is returned. ValidateConfig rejects target == source and digest/invalid targets.
[P3-C] [BUILD] docker_plugin resource (new plugin_resource.go): name (remote ref, RequiresReplace), plugin_reference (computed), alias (optional+computed, RequiresReplace; the daemon's "sshfs:latest" spelling of alias "sshfs" keeps the configured value), enabled (default true, in place), grant_all_permissions, grant_permissions set blocks {name, value set} (ConflictsWith grant_all_permissions), env (optional+computed set, in place via disable -> PluginSet -> enable), enable_timeout (default 60 s), force_destroy, force_disable. Create: client.PluginInstall(alias, {RemoteRef, RegistryAuth from registry_auth, Disabled: true, Args: env, AcceptAllPermissions, AcceptPermissionsFunc}). The client fetches GET /plugins/privileges and passes the list to AcceptPermissionsFunc, which accepts only if every privilege value is granted, otherwise fails before any pull, listing missing and requested privileges. The pull stream is drained with jsonmessage, then PluginEnable runs with enable_timeout. Delete: PluginDisable(force_disable) unless force_destroy, then PluginRemove(force_destroy). Import by ID or name.
[P3-C] [BUILD] docker_plugin data source (new plugin_data_source.go): exactly one of id | alias; returns id, alias, name (familiar ref), plugin_reference, enabled, env via PluginInspectWithRaw.
[P3-C] [ASSUMED] docker_plugin env: the daemon's Settings.Env lists every settable variable with defaults, so when env is configured only the configured keys are reported (all keys on import or when unset). Changing grant_permissions does not reinstall (privileges are only checked at install).

Import by builder name or container ID/name. Nothing shells out.

Services for the swarm acc test are created through the client (not docker_service) so the actions' spec changes do not show as drift in the post-apply plan.
[P3-D] [ASSUMED] Prune filters are map(string), one value per key; accepted keys label, label!, until (others are a validation error). Routing per daemon (daemon/prune.go, daemon/images/image_prune.go, volume/service/service.go): containers/networks/images get label, label!, until; volumes get label, label! only; build cache gets until only. The daemon accepts but ignores label filters for build cache (builder/builder-next/builder.go:198 "TODO(tiborvass): handle label"), which would prune all build cache, so docker_system_prune rejects label/label! filters unless build_cache = false.
[P3-D] [ASSUMED] docker_system_prune defaults mirror `docker system prune`: containers, networks, dangling images and build cache true, volumes false. Extra attribute all_images (= `-a`: images dangling=false and BuildCachePrune All). Volume prune removes anonymous volumes only (API >= 1.42 semantics; no `all` filter exposed).
[P3-D] [ASSUMED] docker_swarm_rotate_tokens defaults worker = true, manager = false (validation error if both false). SwarmInspect -> SwarmUpdate(version, current Spec, UpdateFlags) with version-conflict retry; tokens never logged.
[P3-D] [ASSUMED] docker_node_drain: a task has "left" when its Status.State is complete, shutdown, failed, rejected, remove or orphaned (TaskList filtered by node). wait (default true) and timeout (default 5m) attributes added.
[P3-D] [ASSUMED] Service actions send ServiceUpdateOptions{QueryRegistry: false} (spec image already digest-pinned at create) with EncodedRegistryAuth from registry_auth for the current (or, for rollback, previous) image. force_update and rollback first give the daemon up to 5 polls to report an UpdateStatus whose StartedAt is not older than UpdatedAt-1s, so the wait does not pass on the old tasks before the rollout starts; rollback then waits for rollback_completed + all wanted tasks running (svcConvergeCheck treats rollback states as fatal, so rollback has its own check, actRollbackCheck). Not live-verified.
[P3-D] [ASSUMED] docker_exec timeout ends the action but not the process in the container (the Engine API has no exec kill); documented in the timeout attribute.
[P3-D] [BLOCKED] D docker_node_drain/docker_node_activate/docker_service_scale/docker_service_force_update/docker_service_rollback linked-resource lifecycle actions: terraform-plugin-framework v1.19.0 action package has no linked resources (action/schema has no LinkedResources, InvokeResponse has no state to write back; grep for LinkedResource/Lifecycle in action/ and internal/fwserver/ finds nothing), so an action cannot update the docker_swarm_node / docker_service it acts on. Tried: framework action package and schema types. Implemented the closest version: unlinked actions taking node/service by name or ID; each schema documents that a managing docker_swarm_node (availability) or docker_service (replicas / spec after rollback) will show the change in its next plan.

[P3-F] [ASSUMED] Registry naming: one address must work for the daemon (push/DistributionInspect) and for the provider process (keep_remotely=false DELETE over HTTP). Sharing the manager's network namespace makes 127.0.0.1:12380 valid in both places, and loopback registries are insecure by default in dockerd and get the provider's http fallback (registryImageIsLocal). Workers reach the same registry as manager:12380 (TF_ACC_REGISTRY_NODES, insecure-registry flag). Restarting the manager container would detach the registry's network; no test restarts the manager.

[P3-F] [BUILD] harness-up.{ps1,sh} install Terraform 1.14.9 (HARNESS_TF_VERSION overrides) once into test/harness/.bin/terraform_<ver>/ from releases.hashicorp.com, SHA256-checked against terraform_<ver>_SHA256SUMS, and export TF_ACC_TERRAFORM_VERSION=1.14.9 plus TF_ACC_TERRAFORM_PATH. terraform-plugin-testing v1.16.0 (internal/plugintest/config.go DiscoverConfig) honours TF_ACC_TERRAFORM_PATH first (exact binary, no fallback), then TF_ACC_TERRAFORM_VERSION (hc-install releases.ExactVersion into a fresh temp dir PER TEST), else PATH lookup then latest from checkpoint. Exporting only the version would download ~30 MB for every TestAcc*, hence the cached PATH; if the download fails only TF_ACC_TERRAFORM_VERSION is exported.

Diagnosis: swarmInitBuildRequest (internal/provider/swarm_init_resource.go) sets only InitRequest.AutoLockManagers; dockerd's Init then calls initClusterSpec -> convert.MergeSwarmSpecToGRPC(req.Spec, ...) (daemon/cluster/swarm.go:590, convert/swarm.go:147) which copies req.Spec.EncryptionConfig.AutoLockManagers unconditionally, so the zero Spec turns autolock back off right after init (docker CLI sets both fields). [P3-F] [TODO] PROVIDER BUG docker_service global-mode converge returns before any task runs (TestAccServiceP3_globalMode, full run 1 swarm pass on a fresh harness: "global service: 0 running tasks for 1 eligible nodes (map[assigned:1])"; it passed in the dev run only because busybox was already cached on the manager). Diagnosis: svcConvergeCheck (internal/provider/service_converge.go) sets st.Want = desired for replicas < 0 and then st.Done = st.Running == st.Want; on the first poll right after ServiceCreate/Update the global orchestrator has not created the tasks yet, so desired = running = 0 counts as converged. Pass 1 [L4] ASSUMED "Global mode converges when every task with DesiredState=running is running" is therefore wrong for the empty-task-list case. Exact fix: replace `st.Done = st.Running == st.Want` with `st.Done = st.Running == st.Want && (replicas >= 0 || st.Want > 0)` (a global service whose constraints match no node then times out instead of passing vacuously; say so in converge_config's description), and add a TestServiceConverge case: replicas=-1 with no tasks -> Done=false. Owner: whichever Pass 3 lane owns service_converge.go.

[P3-F] [TODO] PROVIDER BUG (intermittent) docker_swarm_node destroy right after a promote: Pass 2 TestAccSwarmNode_twoWorkersPromote failed in full run 2 swarm pass (passed in run 1 and in Pass 2) with "Cannot remove swarm node: demoting node before leave: updating node <id>: Error response from daemon: rpc error: code = NotFound desc = can't find manager in raft memberlist". Diagnosis: Update (role worker->manager) returns as soon as NodeUpdate is accepted, but the promoted node joins raft asynchronously; the destroy that follows immediately demotes it via swarmUpdateNode (internal/provider/swarm_common.go), and swarmkit rejects the demotion until the node is in the raft member list; swarmUpdateNode retries only version conflicts. Exact fix: in swarm_common.go add func swarmIsRaftPending(err error) bool { return err != nil && strings.Contains(strings.ToLower(err.Error()), "can't find manager in raft memberlist") } and in swarmUpdateNode change `if !swarmIsVersionConflict(err) {` to `if !swarmIsVersionConflict(err) && !swarmIsRaftPending(err) {`, with that case sleeping 2s per attempt and swarmNodeUpdateAttempts raised to cover ~60s (or a separate deadline loop); additionally, after a promote in Create/Update wait (<= swarmJoinTimeout) until NodeInspect shows ManagerStatus != nil && ManagerStatus.Reachability == "reachable", so state is only written once the node really is a manager. Add an httptest case to swarm_node_resource_test.go: first NodeUpdate returns 404 with that message, second 200.

#### Every BLOCKED line, with what was tried

- [P3-C] [BLOCKED] C docker_buildx_builder: builder-instance registration (~/.docker/buildx/instances/<name>; `docker buildx ls`, --use, --append nodes, node --platform) is buildx-private local state with no Engine API endpoint. Tried: the docker v28.5.2 client and moby/buildkit v0.34.2 client expose nothing for it; writing buildx's internal JSON store would depend on an undocumented file format. Container/volume names match buildx's, so `docker buildx create --name <name> --driver docker-container` adopts the node.
- [P3-C] [BLOCKED] C docker_buildx_builder: kubernetes and remote drivers (and buildx's built-in docker driver) are not Engine API objects; `driver` accepts only "docker-container". Tried: nothing applicable exists in the Engine API.
- [P3-D] [BLOCKED] D docker_node_drain / docker_node_activate / docker_service_scale / docker_service_force_update / docker_service_rollback as linked-resource lifecycle actions: terraform-plugin-framework v1.19.0's action package has no linked resources (no LinkedResources in action/schema, no state in InvokeResponse). Tried: framework action package, schema types, grep of action/ and internal/fwserver/. Implemented unlinked actions by name/ID; each schema says the managing resource shows the change on its next plan.
- [P3-F] [BLOCKED] F full-run 4: Docker Hub anonymous pull rate limit (100/h, 0 remaining). Tried: waiting for the window (HEAD probes), then fixed by a pull-through `mirror` cache in the harness (one full run now uses at most 5 Hub pulls). Environmental; resolved.

#### Open TODOs

- Lane A: registry_image_resource.go `registryImageDeleteRemote` uses only Username/Password, so identity-token-only credentials (Docker Hub via the Desktop helper) cannot delete remote tags. Pass IdentityToken as an OAuth refresh token, or document the limit.
- Lane B: compose features not implemented: build, develop/watch, lifecycle hooks, models, provider services, `compose run` one-off containers, pull_policy daily/weekly/refresh. Volume definition changes are warned about, never applied.
- Lane C: docker_image `build.builder_endpoint` (build on a docker_buildx_builder) needs a BuildKit Solve + ImageLoad path (suggested new image_build_remote.go reusing the bxbDialStdio dialer).
- Lane C: container `upload` / `selinux_relabel` are still ASSUMED live; add an acceptance step with an upload block and a z-relabelled bind. Run TestAccPlugin_basic with TF_ACC_PLUGIN=1 on a daemon that can run vieux/sshfs (skipped in both passes).
- Lane E: docker_swarm_node `join_token_wo` + `join_token_wo_version`, so ephemeral docker_swarm_tokens can feed a node join directly.
- Lane F: after a promote, wait for ManagerStatus.Reachability == "reachable" before writing state. The orchestrator added only the raft-memberlist retry in swarmUpdateNode.
- Lane F: still not covered: NodeUpdate version-conflict retry live, default-ulimit merge on import, registry push retry on a transient error, BuildKit edge cases (multi-platform rejection, secrets src file, trace decoding), gpus on a real NVIDIA host.
- Orchestrator: docker_service `converge_config` description should say a global service whose constraints match no node now waits until the timeout.
- Orchestrator: SKILL.md / actions guidance should warn that Terraform 1.14 does not hold a depends_on dependent until the upstream resource's after_create actions finish (seen in TestAccActionImageSaveLoad_basic).
- Orchestrator: the lane worktrees under C:\__Code\tpd-wt\lane{A..G} are still registered. Remove them with `git worktree remove --force C:\__Code\tpd-wt\laneX` (all their owned files are merged here) once this pass is reviewed.

### Pass 4 Lane 0 summary

- **Client migration.** github.com/docker/docker v28.5.2+incompatible was replaced by github.com/moby/moby/client v0.6.2 + github.com/moby/moby/api v1.56.1 (Engine API 1.56). Other modules unchanged: buildkit v0.34.2, docker/cli v29.9.3+incompatible, compose-go v2.16.2.
- **Version policy.** min_api_version (default 1.44, max 1.56) is in the schema and docs. Configure validates it, rejects a pinned api_version below it, and fails when the negotiated daemon version is below it; an unreachable daemon is still only a warning. apiSince/gateAPISince exist but are not wired to any schema yet.
- **Compose hash compatibility.** 134 v28 digests over 12 fixtures (10 YAML files) are pinned in TestComposeHashCompat with 0 mismatches, so upgrading does not recreate compose networks, volumes, containers or services.
- **Debug removal.** The HASH_JSON hook and zz_hash_probe_test.go are gone.
- **Tests** (unit, -short, TF_ACC unset, all packages; counted with go test -json, verdict from Invoke-Build Test):
  - Before this close-out: 295 / 21 / 77 as recorded at the start (and TestZZHashProbe failed without FIXDIR).
  - After: 303 top-level tests pass, 0 fail, 77 skip; 354 passing including 51 subtests. New: 10 min_api_version tests (+6 subtests) and TestComposeHashCompat (+12 subtests); removed: TestZZHashProbe.
- **Gate worklist** for the later Pass 4 lanes: engine:image-attestations -> docker_image_attestations (not implemented), Engine API 1.55. Nothing else in the inventory is above the floor at endpoint level; field-level minimums still need a CHANGELOG pass.
- **Open TODOs:** wire apiFloorHook in client.go; the apiSince annotations above; the nine apiwatch TODOs (to Lane A).

## Pass 5

Goal: lock the naming decisions (reference/DECISIONS.md), then build the full inventory, so that every non-skipped item is implemented, tested and documented. Lane 0 ran serially in the main tree. Lanes A (engine), B (swarm), C (compose + stack), D (dockerfile), E (buildx + BuildKit) and F (migration) then ran in parallel, each in its own worktree under %TEMP%\tpd-p5. The lane worktrees started from one Lane 0 patch, each lane owned a disjoint set of files (reference/_gen/pass5/ownership.json), and each lane's work came back as a patch. Lane Z then integrated the six patches in the main tree. Nothing is committed.

Pass 5 replaces the unrun Pass 4 lanes:
- Pass 4 Lane A (Engine API coverage) went to Pass 5 Lanes A, B, C and E, which built the whole inventory. That includes the nine Pass 4 apiwatch TODOs and the engine:image-attestations gate, now applied through gateItemAPI.
- Pass 4 Lane B (the Pass 3 TODOs) went to the lane that owns each file. The TODOs were not re-checked one by one; the open ones that touched implemented types were covered by the Pass 5 renames, contract and gap work.
- Pass 4 Lane C (release pipeline) was dropped from Pass 5 as out of scope, and stays open.
- Pass 4 Lane D (Claude.Agent dogfood) was dropped from Pass 5 as out of scope, and stays open.
- Pass 4 Lane E (skill, docs, climap, apiwatch) went to Lane Z: GenSkill, Docs, climap at 143 rows, and the genskill layer map.
- Pass 4 Lane F (harness and verification) went to Lane Z, which ran TestAcc. Lane C made the harness change for ComposeParity.

### Pass 5 summary

- **Decisions:** 51 decisions plus the general rule are written in DECISIONS.md and applied to the inventory. The inventory has 252 items: 171 not skipped (28 resources, 55 data sources, 51 actions, 4 ephemeral resources, 8 list resources, 4 functions, 21 Dockerfile blocks), all computed **exists**, and 81 skipped. The provider registers 138 types.
- **Renames:** renames.json has 708 rows: 286 kept, 269 renamed, 153 dropped with a reason, 0 gap. The parity check covers 685 kreuzwerker attributes (532 mapped, 153 dropped with a reason, 0 open) and 26 types (24 have, 2 partial). There is one type rename, data.docker_logs -> data.docker_container_logs. Every kreuzwerker resource type (12) can move its state from registry.terraform.io/kreuzwerker/docker (Terraform >= 1.8). The migration guide is templates/guides/migrating-from-kreuzwerker.md.tmpl.
- **Dependencies:** none added (go.mod/go.sum unchanged). Lane D pulls three more packages from the buildkit v0.34.2 module already required (frontend/dockerfile/{command,shell,parser}).
- **Tests:** unit 556/0/114 (+75 subtests); acceptance plain 71/0/37, swarm 32/0/2.
- **Blocked:** no inventory item is blocked. Sub-features: bake `target:` contexts, cross-target refs, policy and resources; buildx history materials and attachment bodies; the buildx kubernetes/remote drivers and client store, which are not Engine API objects (remote BuildKit endpoints work through the seam).
- **Behaviour changes to note in release notes:**
  - docker_tag.id is the source image ID (state migrated on read).
  - docker_registry_token.token is an object.
  - Plural data sources hold full Engine objects (Q39; e.g. nodes[*].description.hostname).
  - com.docker.compose.config-hash is the CLI's value; the provider digest moved to io.terraform.provider.compose-spec-hash, with no recreation (TestComposeHashCompat passes).
  - remove_orphans defaults to true.
  - Removing docker_network ipam_config is now in place.
  - Some plural *_json outputs and the swarm inspect_json are re-encoded, not the exact bytes, because the moby client returns no raw body for them.
- **Commit groups:** reference/_gen/pass5/commit-groups/ (0-decisions-scaffold, A..F, Z-docs-inventory). They are meant to be committed in order; only the last is guaranteed to build.

## Pass 5.1

Goal: close the gaps the Pass 5 report left open. Lanes H (harness coverage), P (ComposeParity coverage), D (Dockerfile round-trips), R (raw-output fidelity and records) and S (honest status) ran in parallel in the main tree, by the user's instruction for this pass (no worktrees). File ownership was the `pass51` section of reference/_gen/pass5/ownership.json, written by reference/_gen/generators/pass51/p51_ownership.py: every existing file assigned to one lane, prefix rules for new files, and `--check <lane> <listing>` reading the JSON. Each lane kept its changed paths in reference/_gen/pass51/lane-<X>.files and passed `--check` before Lane Z staged. Full lane logs and summaries: reference/_gen/pass51/lane-<X>.md. Nothing is committed.

### Log

The 51 internal/provider test files with TestAcc functions went to H (except compose_parity_acc_c_test.go -> P). R took the contract helpers and every other non-D/S/P internal/provider file from Lanes 0/A/B/C/F, plus the renames files, CONTRACT.md, fragments, tools/parity and templates/guides. S took the Lane E files, merge.py, validate.py and the inventory outputs.
[P5.1-H] [ASSUMED] The brief assumed less infrastructure than already existed. test/harness/docker-compose.yml already had `registry` (registry:2 at 127.0.0.1:12380 / manager:12380, deletes on), `mirror` (Docker Hub pull-through cache), manager, worker1-3 and tlsnode. Three things were actually missing. (a) Nothing was ever seeded into registry:2, so every test pulled from Docker Hub through the mirror. (b) `Invoke-E2E -SwarmInit` only ran `swarm init`. All workers stayed outside the swarm, because the docker_swarm_node / E2E tests join worker1-3 themselves and need them inactive, so the swarm pass never had a ready worker. (c) The harness never exported TF_ACC_PLUGIN, TF_ACC_PLUGIN_DIR or TF_ACC_CSI_PLUGIN, although the skip messages said it did.
[P5.1-H] [BUILD] Registry seeding: test/harness/seed-images.txt lists busybox:1.36, alpine:3.20, nginx:alpine, nginx:1.27-alpine and docker/dockerfile:1. harness-up.ps1 and harness-up.sh pull each on the manager through the mirror, push it as manager:12380/<ref>, remove both local tags (so the manager starts empty) and export TF_ACC_SEEDED_IMAGES.
[P5.1-H] [BUILD] Tests point at the seeded copies through internal/provider/h_harness_images_test.go. `hAccTest` is resource.Test plus a rewrite of every step's Config; `hAccImage`, `hAccRepo`, `hAccHostImage` and `hAccConfig` cover Go-side refs (Dockerfiles, checks). The rewrite is busybox:1.36 -> manager:12380/busybox:1.36, a name that works on the manager and on every worker. Without the harness the Hub ref is returned unchanged. All 109 `resource.Test` calls in TestAcc* functions of the H-owned files now go through hAccTest; Dockerfile and check literals were mapped in compose_acc_c, e2e_acc_p3, image_acc_p3, image_build_buildkit, image_data_source, image_resource, img_acc, reg_registry_acc, registry_image_resource and tag_resource.
[P5.1-H] [BUILD] Unit test TestHAccImageRewrite covers the rewrite rules (qualified refs untouched, longest match first, no partial tag matches).
[P5.1-H] [BUILD] Standby worker: worker4 was added to docker-compose.yml (127.0.0.1:12381, DOCKER_WORKER4_HOST). New scripts/harness-swarm-init.ps1 and .sh run swarm init, join worker4 as a worker, wait for Ready/Active, then create and enable the tfacc-csi plugin on the manager. Invoke-E2E.ps1 -SwarmInit calls the .ps1 script. worker1-3 stay unjoined for the tests that join them.
[P5.1-H] [BUILD] Plugin fixture: test/harness/tfaccplugin/main.go is a test-only static Go binary built with stdlib plus grpc/protobuf, which are already in go.mod; go.mod and go.sum are unchanged. It has two modes. `volume` is a no-op docker.volumedriver/1.0 with a settable DEBUG env. `csi` is a controller-only CSI driver (docker.csicontroller/1.0 + csinode/1.0) that answers the csi.v1 calls swarmkit makes for create, drain and delete, using hand-encoded protobuf via protowire, so the module needs no CSI spec dependency. harness-up builds it with GOOS=linux CGO_ENABLED=0 into test/harness/.plugins/{volume,csi} (config.json + rootfs/, git-ignored, removed by harness-down). It pushes the volume plugin as 127.0.0.1:12380/tfacc/noop-volume:1 and exports TF_ACC_PLUGIN, TF_ACC_PLUGIN_DIR, TF_ACC_CSI_PLUGIN=tfacc-csi and TF_ACC_CSI_PLUGIN_DIR.
[P5.1-H] [ASSUMED] Plugin choice: a self-built static binary rather than a published plugin. It needs no kernel module, no FUSE and no host mount, so it runs inside dind; vieux/sshfs needs /dev/fuse and CAP_SYS_ADMIN on the host kernel. It also comes from source, so neither Docker Hub nor a plugin registry account is needed. CSI is therefore not skipped: the same binary provides a CSI controller that dind can run.
[P5.1-H] [BUILD] TestAccPlugin_basic installs TF_ACC_PLUGIN (a reference) instead of the hard-coded vieux/sshfs. The value "1" still means vieux/sshfs:latest. testAccPluginPreCheck now returns the reference.
[P5.1-H] [BUILD] TestAccObjClusterVolume now calls swarmAccManagerAddr, which it lacked (it is in $SwarmAccRegex), and skips with a written reason when TF_ACC_CSI_PLUGIN is not enabled on DOCKER_HOST.
[P5.1-H] [BUILD] Docker Hub search: harness-up runs `docker search` once from the manager. It exports TPD_ACC_INTERNET=1 when that answers, else TF_ACC_SKIP_INTERNET=1. TestAccImageSearch therefore runs online, anonymously with no credentials, because daemon search goes straight to index.docker.io and registry:2 has no search API. Offline it skips with its existing written reason.
[P5.1-H] [BUILD] TestAccRegistryImageDataSource now reads the harness copy via the host-side name 127.0.0.1:12380/alpine:3.20, with Docker Hub as the fallback. TestAccRegistryTokenEphemeral and TestAccP3RegistryImageDataSources stay on Hub, anonymously, because they test Hub's token service and a Hub multi-arch index; both skip with TF_ACC_SKIP_INTERNET.
[P5.1-H] [BUILD] Plain pass: Invoke-E2E.ps1 gained -Skip, which becomes go test -skip. Invoke-Build TestAcc passes $SwarmAccRegex as -Skip of the plain pass and as -Run of the swarm pass, so the 33 swarm tests run once instead of skipping in the plain pass. I chose this over a written skip reason. A script check confirmed that every TestAcc that calls swarmAccManagerAddr is matched by the regex and that nothing else is.

[P5.1-H] [TODO] R: internal/provider/obj_plugin_actions.go (docker_plugin_create context tar). On a Windows host, tar.FileInfoHeader gives regular files mode 0666, so a plugin created from Windows has a non-executable entrypoint and cannot be enabled. Exact change: after `hdr, err := tar.FileInfoHeader(info, link)`, add `if runtime.GOOS == "windows" && info.Mode().IsRegular() { hdr.Mode |= 0o111 }`. This is the docker CLI's chmodTarEntry behaviour. Found by code reading and not reproduced live: TestAccObjPluginCreatePush only creates and pushes.

All are provider code in compose_*.go, so I left them alone.

[P5.1-P] [VERIFIED] Finding 1 (c2s_webapp, also c2s_webapp_debug in other runs): container webapp-web-{1,2,3} label com.docker.compose.depends_on: cli "db:service_healthy:false,cache:service_started:false", provider "cache:service_started:false,db:service_healthy:false". The CLI writes the label in Go map order (random per container: in run 1 only web-3 differed and c2s_webapp_debug differed on web-1 / web-3; in run 4 c2s_webapp_debug passed), the provider sorts (compose_local_apply.go composeLabelDependsOn). Any service with 2+ depends_on entries fails this check at random.
[P5.1-P] [VERIFIED] Finding 2 (unit_expand): container proj-sidecar-1 label com.docker.compose.config-hash: cli "18549e3e4f5c654fa3ac217fa4e77e9f4e8a1971c94d38cb8af6955d3dfafecf", provider "31e27a3f38b888ab108ee53208ca626492f90b8bd83cea2fab6f696b3c68c147". Verified by hand with the CLI on the harness (two up/down cycles of the same project): the CLI's sidecar hash changes on every run (c2187dcc... then 93e8c69a...) while web's stays 14e25dbd...: the CLI hashes the service after resolving network_mode service:web / volumes_from web to container:<web's id>. The provider's hash is fixed, so `docker compose up` on a provider-made project (or vice versa) recreates the sidecar, and the provider does not recreate the sidecar when web is recreated.
[P5.1-P] [VERIFIED] Finding 3 (unit_expand): container proj-sidecar-1 volumes_from: cli "<proj-web-1>" (the web container id, no mode: `web:ro` is sent read-write), provider "proj-web-1:ro" (container name, read-only kept).
[P5.1-P] [VERIFIED] Finding 4 (unit_expand): container proj-web-1 env: environment `UNSET: null` with UNSET not set in the environment: the CLI sends the bare "UNSET" entry in Config.Env, the provider drops it (cli env ends "...PKG_RELEASE=1~bookworm\nUNSET", provider "...PKG_RELEASE=1~bookworm"). Web's config-hash matches on both sides.

Resolution, per the user's decision: the provider keeps its sorted order (compose_local_apply.go composeLabelDependsOn; the comment now cites create.go:510-513). ComposeParity compares only `label com.docker.compose.depends_on` as an unordered set (composeParitySetLabels / composeParitySetEqual in compose_parity_acc_c_test.go, with a comment citing the same lines). TestComposeParityDiffC asserts three things: the same entries in another order are not a difference, a different set is a difference, and other labels stay order-sensitive.
[P5.2-C] [VERIFIED] Finding 2 (config-hash for service: / volumes_from references): yes. compose v5.1.4 pkg/compose/convergence.go:133 `ensureService` calls `resolveServiceReferences` (:271-283). That calls `resolveVolumeFrom` (:285-303), which turns volumes_from "<svc>[:mode]" into `dependencies.sorted()[0].ID` and "container:<c>[:mode]" into "<c>". It also calls `resolveSharedNamespaces` (:305-320), which turns network_mode / ipc / pid "service:<svc>" into "container:<ID>". The resolved service is then hashed: create.go:497-502 `prepareLabels` -> hash.go:27 `ServiceHash`. `mustRecreate` (convergence.go:326-338) compares the label with that hash. The first container is chosen by name (containers.go:182 `sorted`).
[P5.2-C] [FIXED] Finding 2: new internal/provider/c_compose_refs.go (composeLocalRefs) replicates resolveVolumeFrom / resolveSharedNamespaces on a copy of the service. composeLocalExpand stores the refs on each container (composeLocalContainer.Refs, `json:"-"`, not part of the spec hash). At create time, composeLocalCreate -> composeLocalResolveRefs inspects the target containers by name and does two things: it writes config-hash = composeServiceConfigHash(resolved service), and it sends NetworkMode / IpcMode / PidMode "container:<ID>" and VolumesFrom "<ID>[:mode]", as compose does (create.go:298-319). The plan keeps container names, so the spec hash, and with it TestComposeHashCompat, is unchanged.
[P5.2-C] [FIXED] Finding 2, convergence: the provider now matches compose's behaviour, because composeLocalApply recreates a container with references when either of these holds: (a) a target container is created or recreated in the same apply (the new `replaced` set, checked by container name like compose's first container, so scaling the target up does not count); (b) its config-hash label differs from the hash resolved against the live target IDs. Check (b) applies only to containers that carry the spec-hash label; older ones keep the composeHashMatches rule. composeLocalDrift reports the same condition on refresh ("container X was created for a container of Y that has been replaced"), so a target replaced out of band shows as an update plan. A dependency whose only link is volumes_from (restart: false) is now recreated too, as in compose.

The only difference from chmodTarEntry: Lane Z keeps setuid/setgid/sticky (chmodTarEntry clears them); a Windows os.FileMode never carries them, so the two are identical for every header objTarDir builds (TestWLaneZFixVsChmodTarEntry over 0..0o7777; TestWObjTarDirModes asserts exact equality per entry on Windows). - **The four ComposeParity findings (compose v5.1.4 @ 4732a2ed, compose-go v2.11.0):**
  1. depends_on label order: verified (pkg/compose/create.go:510-513, prepareLabels, ranges over the DependsOn map). The provider keeps its sorted order; ComposeParity compares that one label as an unordered set, with the source cited in the test.
  2. config-hash of service: / volumes_from references: verified (convergence.go:133 ensureService -> resolveServiceReferences :271-320, resolveVolumeFrom :285-303, resolveSharedNamespaces :305-320; hash.go:27 ServiceHash; mustRecreate :326-338). Fixed: local mode resolves the references to container IDs at create time, hashes the resolved service, and recreates dependants when a target is recreated. TestComposeHashCompat passes with goldens unchanged.
  3. volumes_from `:ro`: verified (resolveVolumeFrom keeps only the ID; create.go:309). The compose-spec documents ro/rw. The provider keeps `:ro` (security); it is the one allowlisted ComposeParity difference, documented in reference/CONTRACT.md.
  4. Null environment entries: verified (convert.go:30-40 ToMobyEnv; compose-go types/mapping.go:55-64). Fixed: bare KEY sent to the Engine; unit test TestComposeFakeLocalRefsC plus the unit_expand fixture.

## Pass 5.3

Goal: a field-level coverage gate (every writable Engine API, compose-spec and bake field mapped, skipped with a reason, or partial), the agreed review items (owner labels, per-call API logging), the Pass 5.2 open decisions D1-D10, and the partials. Lane 0 ran serially in the main tree. Lanes A (engine/container), R (registry/auth), S (swarm), K (compose), B (buildx) and I (CI/docs) ran in parallel, one git worktree each under %TEMP%	pd-p53, detached at 49e65d7 with the Lane 0 patch applied to the index (tree 301b9e0ec616). Lane Z integrated in the main tree. Ownership: the `pass53` section of reference/_gen/pass5/ownership.json. Each lane's full log and summary: reference/_gen/pass53/lane-<X>.md. Nothing is committed.

Installed through the moby client's WithHTTPRequestHook / WithHTTPResponseHook, not by wrapping the transport: moby clones a given *http.Client and needs its own base *http.Transport for SSH dialing, TLS hijack and idle-connection close (moby/client v0.6.2 client_options.go WithHTTPClient, client.go baseTransport / dialerFromTransport), and its hookTransport also covers hijacked requests (hijack.go). [P5.3-A] [BUILD] D1 docker_container `state` = running | created | exited (Docker `State.Status` names), internal/provider/ctr_p53.go + container_resource.go. Default `running`; running<->created in place, to/from `exited` forces replacement (ctrStateReplaceIf).
[P5.3-A] [BUILD] D1 `must_run` removed; schema version 0 -> 1 with a JSON state upgrader (ctrUpgradeV0): drops must_run, sets state = "running", fills expected_exit_codes / logs_tail defaults, prunes keys the current schema lacks at every level (attributes earlier passes removed without a version bump).
[P5.3-A] [BUILD] D1 state = "exited": create, start, ContainerWait(not-running) within wait_timeout (default 5m for exited, 60s otherwise; set in ModifyPlan when the config leaves it unset), then ContainerLogs (tail = logs_tail, default 1000). Outputs exit_code, finished_at (State.FinishedAt), logs + log_entries (shapes and sensitivity reused from the engine:container-logs contract). Exit code outside expected_exit_codes (default [0]) fails the apply with the last 20 lines; the container stays in state (tainted). A refresh keeps the captured output and never starts it again; a missing container is removed from state (plan to create).
[P5.3-A] [BUILD] D1 state = "created": never started by the provider; found running, ModifyPlan adds a warning and plans no change. state = "running": a stopped container is planned running=true and started in place (unchanged Pass 2 behaviour).
[P5.3-A] [BUILD] D1 validation (ValidateConfig): auto_remove + exited is an error pointing at docker_container_run; wait = true needs state = "running"; restart other than "no" with exited is an error (it would restart the one-shot run).
[P5.3-A] [BUILD] D1 files block: in-place re-upload now only for a stopped container with state = "created" (was must_run = false).

[P5.3-R] [BUILD] docker_image_commit: new `config` attribute, the ImageCommit body (`ContainerConfig`, swagger reference/upstream/engine-api/api/swagger.yaml:10887-10891, definition :1338). All 24 ContainerConfig fields under their snake_case upstream names (hostname, domainname, user, attach_stdin/stdout/stderr, exposed_ports, tty, open_stdin, stdin_once, env, cmd, healthcheck{test,interval,timeout,start_period,start_interval,retries}, args_escaped, image, volumes, working_dir, entrypoint, network_disabled, on_build, labels, stop_signal, stop_timeout, shell) written into container.Config (img_action_commit.go imgCommitExpandConfig). ValidateConfig checks ports and healthcheck durations.
[P5.3-R] [VERIFIED] Daemon semantics documented in the attribute: a non-nil body replaces the container config, then merge() fills User, ExposedPorts, Env (by key), Labels, Cmd/Entrypoint, Healthcheck, WorkingDir, Volumes, StopSignal from the container; OnBuild and Shell are not merged (module cache github.com/moby/moby@v28.5.2+incompatible/daemon/commit.go:25-120, :156-162). The graphdriver image store keeps the whole container.Config (image/image.go:68-69), the containerd store the image-config subset (daemon/containerd/imagespec.go:73-99), so every field is persisted by at least one store; none skipped.
[P5.3-R] [BUILD] docker_image_load: new `quiet` (ImageLoad `quiet`, swagger :11169), default true (previous behaviour); false forwards per-layer progress (client moby/client@v0.6.2/image_load.go:29-32).
[P5.3-R] [BUILD] docker_registry_image: new `platform` (ImagePush `platform`, swagger :10458), `os[/arch[/variant]]`, RequiresReplace ("Forces replacement."), apiSince("1.46") with pd.gateAPISince in ModifyPlan (client requiresVersion "1.46": moby/client@v0.6.2/image_push.go:52-53). The graphdriver daemon checks the image matches the platform (daemon/images/image_push.go:20-23).
[P5.3-R] [BUILD] D9: data docker_registry_image reads the registry directly when insecure_skip_verify is set (true: TLS verification off, false: verified), through the existing Registry v2 client (datasource_p3_registry.go newP3Registry + reg_manifests.go regInspect, shallow), and builds the same DistributionInspect shape the daemon returns (descriptor of the top manifest; platforms of an index's entries, or of a single manifest's config, as daemon api/server/router/distribution/distribution_routes.go:134-155). Unset: through the daemon as before. The API gate (DistributionInspect 1.30) now only applies to the daemon path.
[P5.3-R] [FIXED] datasource_p3_registry.go send(): a certificate verification error on a loopback registry no longer falls back to plain http (which turned it into an "HTTP request to an HTTPS server" 400 and hid the certificate error). regIsTLSVerifyError.
[P5.3-R] [FIXED] Removed `// PARTIAL(pass5) engine:registry-image-data ...` from internal/provider/pass51_partial_s.go (feature implemented); merge.py will compute status `exists`.
[P5.3-R] [BUILD] D4: precedence implemented and documented: registry_auth block (username/password, then DOCKER_REGISTRY_USER/PASS, then its docker config) > for registries without a block, the default docker config (DOCKER_CONFIG, else ~/.docker/config.json; new dockerConfigAuth in provider_auth.go, read on first use, cached per registry, unresolved = anonymous) > anonymous. In a config: credHelpers[registry] > credsStore > auths (credentialFromDockerConfig, unchanged). Used by registryAuthConfigFor / registryAuthFor (every pull/push/service/compose path that calls them) and p3RegistryCreds (direct registry reads).
[P5.3-R] [ASSUMED] D4 decision: docker/cli's credentials package is kept (already a dependency; NativeStore runs the helper through docker-credential-helpers/client, which IS the protocol: `get`, server URL on stdin, JSON on stdout, not-found message: module cache docker-credential-helpers@v0.9.9/client/client.go:47-53, docker/cli@v29.9.3/cli/config/credentials/native_store.go:43, :119-132). An in-tree copy would duplicate it with no gain. The allow_credential_helpers gate (CLAUDE.md standing rule) is kept for both block and default-config lookups.
[P5.3-R] [TEST] TestCredentialHelperProtocolR / TestDockerConfigAuthDefaultR build internal/provider/testdata/credhelper as docker-credential-tfacc(.exe) into t.TempDir(), put it first on PATH (t.Setenv) and check stdin protocol, "<token>" identity token, not-found -> auths, error naming the helper, explicit credentials first, the gate (no run when false), caching and anonymous fallback.

No conflict check, no adopt. TestImageActionsOwnerLabelR covers set/unset for both.
